      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/CORE_RESOLUTION_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 15877,
      "sha256": "69d0e64394de58c4d86a3ec57cf282978d131a1a9c1eaa21632bb7d3b72562da",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 35,
        "last": 60
      },
      "evidenceText": "## Resolve a damage event\n\nResolve each genuinely separate hit or damage event once. Do not split one packet to multiply Wound saves or combine separate hits merely to cross a threshold. Feature-specific prevention that acts before damage or before reaching zero resolves at its printed timing first.\n\n1. Resolve attack/save, damage and applicable defenses. The remaining nonnegative amount is **resolved damage**.\n2. Record HP, Wounds and zero-HP state before the event. Remove temporary HP first. **Post-temp damage = resolved damage minus temporary HP absorbed.** Keep this amount even when it exceeds current HP; floor the resulting current HP at zero.\n3. Check V6 Instant Death. If post-temp damage minus HP before the hit is at least maximum HP, the creature dies. For a creature already at zero, this is post-temp damage at least maximum HP. Stop: there is no Wound save, death-save failure or successful knockout from that event.\n4. Otherwise, a living creature makes one Wound save if it dropped from positive HP to zero, if **2 × post-temp damage is at least maximum HP**, or if the effect actually inflicts Maiming. Overlapping triggers still cause only one save. The post-temp threshold and one-save rule are new provisional interpretations. Maiming is an independent trigger even when temporary HP absorbs all ordinary damage; immunity to Maiming itself still applies.\n5. Make a Constitution save against **max(10, floor(post-temp damage / 2))**. Use pre-event Wounds for the -1-per-Wound penalty. Maiming with zero post-temp damage uses DC 10. A Wound gained now cannot penalize the roll that created it.\n6. A success adds no Wound. A failure that began with zero, one or two Wounds adds exactly one; failure by 5 or more also gives that Wound one Injury. A failure that began with three Wounds kills the creature instead: no fourth Wound and no Injury roll. Stop processing later living-target consequences if dead.\n7. If the creature was already at zero before this event and post-temp damage is positive, end Stable. A creature using death saves adds one failure, or two for a critical hit; three failures kill it. An ordinary creature that does not use death saves dies instead, without starting death counters. A hit from positive HP to zero does not also cause a same-packet death failure.\n8. If this event newly reduces positive HP to zero, a valid melee knockout makes the surviving creature Stable. Otherwise a character/significant NPC using death saves begins dying; an ordinary monster dies. A previously Stable creature whose temporary HP absorbed all damage remains Stable unless an independent Maiming result killed it. The Director identifies significant NPCs consistently. Knockout cannot override Instant Death or a fatal Wound save.\n\nCore's Maiming and Wound trigger rules remain subject to an actual feature's eligibility. The reference helper receives already legal events; it does not grant every attack Maiming or a damage-prevention exception.\n\n## Zero HP and recovery\n\nA living character at zero HP is Unconscious. Core's condition already includes Incapacitated, unawareness, dropping held items and falling Prone; an adjacent hit is critical as specified there. Healing does not silently remove other independent causes of Unconscious or Prone.\n\nWhile dying, make one death save at the **start of each regular initiative turn**. Roll d20 without modifiers unless a feature specifically modifies death saves. Natural 20 restores 1 HP; natural 1 adds two failures. Other results of 10 or more add one success; 9 or less add one failure. Three successes make the creature Stable; three failures make it Dead. Extra turns and Actions add no extra death saves. Stable or Dead creatures do not roll them.\n\nRegaining positive HP or becoming Stable resets both counters. Temporary HP does not raise normal HP, end Unconscious or reset counters. Fully absorbed damage does not end Stable or add a death failure in this provisional interpretation. Independent Maiming can still cause its Wound save.\n\n**Stabilize:** an Action and DC 10 Wisdom (Medicine) check, or an Action using one healer's-kit use automatically, stabilizes a living dying creature at zero. It remains Unconscious and makes no death saves. Roll 1d4 once when it becomes Stable and record its absolute recovery time that many hours later. If still Stable then, it regains 1 HP. Damage reaching normal HP cancels that deadline and resumes dying with the appropriate failures. Rest and Stable recovery use the same elapsed clock, so four hours of recovery inside an eight-hour Watch do not cost twelve hours. A later rest does not separately replay the Stable healing event.\n\n**Dead:** ordinary healing and stabilization do not restore life. Resurrection must satisfy its own willing-soul, body, time, cost and target rules. It does not automatically remove Wounds, Corruption, debts, legal death or claims upon the body. Corpses cannot supply repeated resource triggers.",
      "role": "One Wound on the stated failure; actual zero/recipient lifecycle",
      "descriptiveTopic": "Resolve a damage event / Zero HP and recovery",
      "sectionReferenceType": "Descriptive topic; use the exact supplied path/hash and line range when present, not a claimed verbatim heading"
    },
    {
      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/COMBAT_ACTION_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 30409,
      "sha256": "f90d039142145fbd31c37992ebece70d7926dc4b6081e340f9a2fd9dc7237bc6",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 154,
        "last": 180
      },
      "evidenceText": "## Movement and geometry\n\nTrack **spent movement cost**, not distance, for ordinary turn movement. Mode cap = `(1 + number of legal Dash actions this turn) × current Speed for that mode`; remaining = max(0, cap − spent cost). Switching modes never refunds cost. Recalculate cap on Speed changes, preserving spent cost. Stand from Prone costs floor(half current Speed) and requires nonzero Speed and enough budget. Dropping Prone costs no movement.\n\nFor each foot traveled, normal/difficult/severe terrain costs 1/2/3; use strongest terrain only. Add +1 for crawling and +1 for squeezing. Crawl in difficult =3:1; crawl+squeeze in difficult =4:1. A nonhostile occupied space counts as difficult terrain. Enter a hostile creature's space only with ≥two-size difference or an express feature; never willingly end in an occupied space. Squeeze through a space one size smaller, with own attack/DEX-save disadvantage and attacks-against advantage.\n\nWalk10 through difficult spends20; switch to Fly30 leaves10. Stand with Speed30 spends15; switching to Fly30 leaves15. Two legal Dashes at Speed30 give cap90; prior expenditure still counts. Interpose/Press grant5 budget: they cannot enter a difficult 5-ft tile costing10. Their separate budgets never refill ordinary movement. Ready movement uses Speed at release; Withdraw uses half current Speed. All voluntary movement requires nonzero relevant Speed.\n\nForced movement does not use these ledgers or provoke ordinary opportunity attacks, cannot place creatures in solid matter, and stops at obstacles. Teleport does not traverse intervening terrain or spend Speed unless printed; it requires a legal destination. Speed0 alone does not block forced movement or teleport. Flight with Prone/Speed0/lost support causes Falling unless Hover or an explicit magical support exception applies; use the existing fixed Fall Marker procedure. Reaching support ends Falling.\n\nNEW grid metric: each orthogonal or diagonal neighboring tile is 5 ft; use the same metric for ordinary ranges/areas. No diagonal cut through a corner when both adjacent orthogonal sides are completely blocked. Exact templates, 3D geometry, mounts, chases, jumping, climbing/swimming procedures and currents need further source review.\n\n## Cover, sight, Hide and Search\n\nKeep **visibility**, **known location**, and **line of effect** separate. Seeing through a barrier does not itself permit attacking through it. Best cover only: half +2 AC/DEX saves; three-quarters +5; total prevents direct targeting through that obstruction. Never add +2 and +5. For areas, evaluate line/cover from the area's origin to each target; total cover there excludes a creature unless the effect explicitly travels around/through the obstruction. The origin point itself must be legally targetable.\n\nWithin normal ranged range, no range penalty; beyond normal through long, disadvantage; beyond long, illegal. Ranged attacks also have disadvantage when within5ft of a non-Incapacitated hostile that can see the attacker, even if the attacker cannot see that hostile. All advantage/disadvantage follows the single cancellation rule.\n\nNEW **Hide**: permitted only against observers unable to see you clearly or where a feature permits. Roll DEX Stealth once, record natural die and total. Against each observer's passive DC `10 + WIS Perception modifier including applicable proficiency`, use the Core four-degree task system. Meet DC succeeds before natural1/20 degree adjustment; Success/Critical Success establishes Hidden for that observer. No unsourced passive ±5 adjustment for advantage/disadvantage. Actual Search rolls use their normal modifiers.\n\nHidden means that observer cannot see you and does not know your location. Clear sight or learning your position ends Hidden for that observer. Moving between hiding places can retain Hidden only if no clear sight/revealing event occurs. A failed Hide cannot simply be rerolled unchanged; a materially new hiding position, concealment, distraction, sight-breaking event or express reroll feature is required. Hidden is not a generic immunity or a free stealth roll for every move.\n\n**Search** Action: WIS Perception for sensory detection; INT Investigation for appropriate traces/deduction; Core Arcana/Engineering where relevant. Directly locating a Hidden creature defaults to Perception against its recorded final Stealth total as a static DC, using task degrees/natural shifts. Success reveals location only to the searcher and ends Hidden for that observer, without automatically granting sight. Communicate normally to inform others. Search can be repeated on later turns by paying the Action.\n\nAttacks against unseen targets have disadvantage; unseen attackers have advantage, subject to the **Core Invisible exception**: once its position is known, Invisible's own attack modifiers end for that observer and cannot immediately return solely because it remains Invisible. Independent darkness, smoke, blindness or hiding can still supply modifiers.\n\nResolve an attack using pre-attack information; afterward reveal location to observers able to perceive its evidence. If an unseen target's location is unknown, do not directly choose it for a creature-target effect. A weapon/projectile can instead attack a suspected space: wrong space automatically misses; correct space uses unseen rules. A “creature you can see” requirement is never met by position knowledge alone. Area effects can target legal points under their own rules.",
      "role": "Real terrain/crawl/squeeze cost and forced movement",
      "descriptiveTopic": "Movement, terrain, cover and perception",
      "sectionReferenceType": "Descriptive topic; use the exact supplied path/hash and line range when present, not a claimed verbatim heading"
    },
    {
      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/CREATURE_LIFECYCLE_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 20087,
      "sha256": "eae68470d4dba14aa74334e22270e35a8aea022d3c5013aa75a96c15d8e93b08",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 13,
        "last": 45
      },
      "evidenceText": "Use the owner's original regular companion markers for recharge and previous/next-turn references. Extra turns, initiative changes and removal do not restart those markers. The attached phase's event-resolution checkpoint is distinct: it refreshes no Reaction, recharge, Action, death save or regular duration clock.\n\n## Different kinds of creature life\n\n| Creature | HP and 0 HP | Return and identity |\n|---|---|---|\n| Finite standard summons, Druid swarms, Mage Familiar, Mirror Beasts | HP-only bounded layer; at 0 disappear/end | No corpse, resource or free resummon; a new summon pays its actual source costs |\n| Ranger Beast | HP-only; **NEW** immediate death at 0, no Wound checks, Stable or death saves | Actual animal and ordinary surviving corpse; death ends bond. No automatic resurrection or rebond |\n| Mage Lich elite | Destroyed bound asset at 0; no usable corpse/resources | Same unique asset remains owned. Soulbound or actual completed LR can return it |\n| Wizard standard undead | Destroyed at 0; consumed body never returns as new fuel | Ends on destruction, dismissal or owner's next completed LR, including Auto-Raised minions |\n| Wizard elite | Supplied profile/cap only in this package | Binding/acquisition and return procedure are still missing; no create/bind/return API |\n| Reflective Companion | Original creature's HP and life rules | Temporary control relation; no copied body, reset, new statistics or duplicate turn |\n| Druid form | Same character's HP/life rules | Full forms and Buffer Reserve remain a separate open interface |\n\nCompanion healing restores legal normal HP only. It does not reset identity, duration, recharge, actions, source uses, effects or command budgets. Ranger's “heal normally” supplies no Hit Dice pool or automatic rest healing. The HP-only layer is specific to this reviewed companion chassis, not generic law for all animals/NPCs. Existing animal conditions, disease, poison, explicit injuries and timed effects survive bonding.\n\n## Stats and ownership\n\n**NEW:** finite created creatures snapshot base HP and AC at creation. Attacks, saves, class DC and S use the current values of the original source owner at resolution. Keep source owner, current controller, source class and creation source separate. A temporary controller does not donate their class levels, PB or attack bonus. The implementation rejects stats belonging to another owner/class; it provides no unrestricted transfer/rebinding operation.\n\nStandard profile: AC 12 + floor(PB/2), HP 2L, Speed 30, owner PB for saves, one class attack for S d6 with printed melee/ranged mode. Druid swarm uses current PB+WIS, piercing and its printed tiny-gap movement. Familiar snapshots AC 12+PB and HP 5+Mage level+M; its current source-owner Mage attack fires a 30-foot Elemental Dart for S d4. Focus is fixed at creation: Lightning adds Fly30, Fire/Cold their matching resistance. Changing Focus neither morphs nor refills it. One Familiar; replacement ends the prior one.\n\nRanger maximum is **NEW dynamic** max(1, 2×current Ranger level + actual WIS modifier), not M. Current PB updates its standard AC/saves/attack. Persistent Mage elite maxima use current source-owner Mage level; fixed AC stays fixed. Capacity growth never heals; shrink clamps HP without damage. Wizard elite profiles use the same names with their own original Wizard source statistics if a future legitimate binding source establishes them.\n\nThe [reviewed maximum-HP ledger](MAXIMUM_HP_PLAYTEST.md) supplies source-qualified modifiers. Conjuration is the original creating Wizard level snapshot and requires actual Wizard spell creation. Corpse Harvest qualifies only with actual access; Auto-Raise is not a spell and grants no new Conjuration snapshot. Flesh Ascendant is the explicit dynamic eligible-current-controller exception; engine and card are one effect, not two bonuses. Creation starts at full final legal maximum unless the actual source enters damaged; later added capacity never fills HP. Callers update dynamic modifier records from their real sources.\n\n## Ranger's persistent bond\n\nUse the printed one-hour ritual, touch, 4 RP and one long-rest use. The animal must already be willing/tamed, noncorrupted, INT below7 and CR no greater than Ranger level. The helper does not invent finding/taming rules. Keep one chosen movement mode, capped40 feet, and the original keen-sense identity; no monster stat block, boss powers or free special actions. Exact keen-sense selection definitions remain an evidence gap.\n\n**NEW initial conversion:** for a living animal, new HP = max(1, floor(new maximum × old current HP / old maximum)). A wounded animal stays proportionally wounded. This is profile conversion, not healing or damage. A living bound Beast cannot be rebonded to refill HP. On death, ordinary legal resurrection may restore the same animal under its own source, but it remains unbonded. A new full ritual also requires an actual completed long rest after bond death.\n\nPhysical body identity is separate from current corpse eligibility. Keep one record whose state is living, corpse, consumed or removed. Accepting an already-resolved, independently legal resurrection immediately marks that same body living and unavailable as a corpse, before any rebond. Corpse Gateway cannot use it while living. A later genuine death with a surviving body makes that same record a corpse again; no fragment/new body identity is generated. A consumed/removed body requires explicit reconstruction by the actual resurrection source before it can return. The notification helper grants no resurrection ability, payment exemption or automatic bond. Rebond uses the actual post-resurrection HP ratio and preserves death history; it cannot claim a healthier ratio. Full global corpse decay/cleanup remains open.",
      "role": "HP-only ordinary death versus source-specific retained body",
      "descriptiveTopic": "Different kinds of creature life / Ranger Beast companion",
      "sectionReferenceType": "Descriptive topic; use the exact supplied path/hash and line range when present, not a claimed verbatim heading"
    },
    {
      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/COSMOLOGY_EPILOGUE_RELIC_NULL_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 59409,
      "sha256": "de147857b37ddf3e5632b067023e5863214e4c64bcee9d7a4f1190ab59beae83",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 75,
        "last": 88
      },
      "evidenceText": "**Passive Insight.** While Veil is active and not suppressed, final passive Insight is 5. This is a setting of that passive score, not omniscience or motive detection. Suppression restores normal calculation for its duration.\n\n**Creation-time nonlethal damage flag.** At creation of each damage root, record root dealer. If that dealer is a character currently under unsuppressed Null Veil, mark the entire root `nullNonlethal=true`. Descendant delayed/ongoing packets inherit the creation-time flag; later Veil changes do not rewrite it. A distinct later activation/attack creates a new root and re-evaluates. A companion/minion has its own dealer identity. Controller Veil does not convert companion damage unless an exact ability says the controller is the damage dealer. Pure transfer/redirection keeps the original root's nullNonlethal flag. It does not create a second root or extra prevention/resource triggers. An effect that explicitly creates new reflected/retaliatory damage uses the reflector as dealer and evaluates that new root at creation. A mere transfer is not reflection.\n\nWhen a root is qualifying Null-nonlethal, use this order:\n\n1. Snapshot recipient identity/lifecycle, pre-event HP/tempHP/Wounds/death state and the root nullNonlethal flag. Resolve ordinary defenses, resistance and temp-HP absorption and calculate the ordinary post-temp damage/HP loss values; attached non-damage riders remain pending under their own triggers.\n2. If nullNonlethal is false, use the ordinary CORE/HEALING_DEATH/lifecycle procedure unchanged. If true, continue below. Objects/noncreature structures still take ordinary object damage and can be destroyed; the word nonlethal does not make objects indestructible.\n3. For any living-recipient qualifying damage packet, suppress damage-caused Instant Death and suppress the packet’s Core Wound/Maiming save entirely, including half-maximum and positive-HP Wound triggers. Therefore the packet cannot create a fatal fourth-Wound result while the recipient remains above 0 or reaches 0. Existing Wounds remain recorded.\n4. Apply normal HP loss down to minimum 0. Positive→positive: stop the Null zero-state branch; recipient keeps resulting positive HP, existing Wounds/conditions and independent riders. Positive→0 ordinary living: skip damage-caused death/death-save failures and establish Stable0 with counters reset and exactly one ordinary Stable recovery deadline, unless a source-specific zero-HP permission such as actual Undying Spellform applies as stated below.\n5. Already Dying0: qualifying damage causes no new damage failure/death and does not stabilize, clear counters or move the existing death-save schedule. Already Stable0: causes no failure/death and keeps the existing recovery deadline exactly; never reroll/extend it. If temp HP absorbs the entire normal-HP loss so HP never reaches0, no zero-state transition occurs.\n6. For nonliving/companion/projection recipients, use exactly one explicit recipientLifecycle branch below; do not infer anatomy from a name. The Null override replaces only the damage-caused lethal/destruction endpoint named by that branch. Independent duration, dismissal, concentration/source end, sacrifice/cost, Hunt finality or other non-damage expiry remains real.\n7. Would-die/death-trigger protections do not trigger or spend if their prerequisite death/fatal branch was skipped by Null. A source-specific trigger keyed only to damage reducing HP to0 without killing, such as actual Undying Spellform, still triggers. Independent non-damage riders resolve if their own trigger remains true and they do not require a Wound/death result.\n8. Direct HP payments/sacrificial costs are not damage and are never converted. New reflected/retaliatory damage that is a new root re-evaluates its own dealer; pure transfer/redirection preserves the original root flag and does not duplicate root triggers.",
      "role": "Actual dealer/creation-time flag and Null-disabled bodies",
      "descriptiveTopic": "Null damage roots and recipient lifecycles",
      "sectionReferenceType": "Descriptive topic; use the exact supplied path/hash and line range when present, not a claimed verbatim heading"
    }
  ],
  "procedurePointer": "/spells/21/procedure",

}

A different nearby water region contains an actual immersed body with its own5x5 collision silhouette and a clear vertical water route to the exposed surface.",
    "immersedBody": "Existing significant living NPC HP100/max100, CON total18, no cold defense; geometry/custody already established",
    "additionalBranch": "An independent comparison branch places a real solid obstruction above that body in the selected-water clearance; this branch changes geometry before the cast, not after results."
  },
  "orderedAdjudication": [
    "At t0 commit Action/V-S-F/13 RP, RP40->27; no Counterspell or other active price/source hook is assumed. Choose the actual point and original water/recipient snapshots.",
    "Shared10d6=31. Caster CON natural15+2=17 succeeds, takes15 cold:68->53. Immersed NPC CON18 succeeds, takes15:100->85. Neither reaches zero or its half-max Wound threshold; no ongoing concentration save applies.",
    "Resolve actual life/body consequences, then subtract the extant body/object silhouettes and the selected vertical clearance from only the original water snapshot. The separate1000-lb section freezes in place; body fluids and new flow are absent from the water set.",
    "In the clear-route branch the body column remains water, not air or a granted movement path. In the obstructed comparison branch the original selected-water connected component touching that body stays unfrozen; the disconnected1000-lb section can still freeze. No shortest-path search through the obstruction occurs."
  ],
  "expectedState": {
    "RP": 27,
    "casterHP": 53,
    "NPC_HP": 85,
    "coldDamageCompleteAt": 0,
    "physicalIce": "One actual section remains a conserved material lot after the instantaneous spell; no continuing freeze/re-scan/concentration",
    "clearanceConsequence": "No new encasement route, support, air, breathing or forced movement is granted; preexisting hazards remain."
  },
  "sourceRefs": [
    {
      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/data/public/spell_playtests/catalog.json",
      "bytes": 4945319,
      "sha256": "f52cf6a4a4673897615a74c7c67dcba798c431362d4357e71ee552cc7c5d31f8",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "pointer": "/spells/472",
      "resolvedIdentity": "freezing-sphere",
      "role": "Complete original source/access for Freezing Sphere"
    },
    {
      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/CORE_RESOLUTION_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 15877,
      "sha256": "69d0e64394de58c4d86a3ec57cf282978d131a1a9c1eaa21632bb7d3b72562da",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 35,
        "last": 60
      },
      "evidenceText": "## Resolve a damage event\n\nResolve each genuinely separate hit or damage event once. Do not split one packet to multiply Wound saves or combine separate hits merely to cross a threshold. Feature-specific prevention that acts before damage or before reaching zero resolves at its printed timing first.\n\n1. Resolve attack/save, damage and applicable defenses. The remaining nonnegative amount is **resolved damage**.\n2. Record HP, Wounds and zero-HP state before the event. Remove temporary HP first. **Post-temp damage = resolved damage minus temporary HP absorbed.** Keep this amount even when it exceeds current HP; floor the resulting current HP at zero.\n3. Check V6 Instant Death. If post-temp damage minus HP before the hit is at least maximum HP, the creature dies. For a creature already at zero, this is post-temp damage at least maximum HP. Stop: there is no Wound save, death-save failure or successful knockout from that event.\n4. Otherwise, a living creature makes one Wound save if it dropped from positive HP to zero, if **2 × post-temp damage is at least maximum HP**, or if the effect actually inflicts Maiming. Overlapping triggers still cause only one save. The post-temp threshold and one-save rule are new provisional interpretations. Maiming is an independent trigger even when temporary HP absorbs all ordinary damage; immunity to Maiming itself still applies.\n5. Make a Constitution save against **max(10, floor(post-temp damage / 2))**. Use pre-event Wounds for the -1-per-Wound penalty. Maiming with zero post-temp damage uses DC 10. A Wound gained now cannot penalize the roll that created it.\n6. A success adds no Wound. A failure that began with zero, one or two Wounds adds exactly one; failure by 5 or more also gives that Wound one Injury. A failure that began with three Wounds kills the creature instead: no fourth Wound and no Injury roll. Stop processing later living-target consequences if dead.\n7. If the creature was already at zero before this event and post-temp damage is positive, end Stable. A creature using death saves adds one failure, or two for a critical hit; three failures kill it. An ordinary creature that does not use death saves dies instead, without starting death counters. A hit from positive HP to zero does not also cause a same-packet death failure.\n8. If this event newly reduces positive HP to zero, a valid melee knockout makes the surviving creature Stable. Otherwise a character/significant NPC using death saves begins dying; an ordinary monster dies. A previously Stable creature whose temporary HP absorbed all damage remains Stable unless an independent Maiming result killed it. The Director identifies significant NPCs consistently. Knockout cannot override Instant Death or a fatal Wound save.\n\nCore's Maiming and Wound trigger rules remain subject to an actual feature's eligibility. The reference helper receives already legal events; it does not grant every attack Maiming or a damage-prevention exception.\n\n## Zero HP and recovery\n\nA living character at zero HP is Unconscious. Core's condition already includes Incapacitated, unawareness, dropping held items and falling Prone; an adjacent hit is critical as specified there. Healing does not silently remove other independent causes of Unconscious or Prone.\n\nWhile dying, make one death save at the **start of each regular initiative turn**. Roll d20 without modifiers unless a feature specifically modifies death saves. Natural 20 restores 1 HP; natural 1 adds two failures. Other results of 10 or more add one success; 9 or less add one failure. Three successes make the creature Stable; three failures make it Dead. Extra turns and Actions add no extra death saves. Stable or Dead creatures do not roll them.\n\nRegaining positive HP or becoming Stable resets both counters. Temporary HP does not raise normal HP, end Unconscious or reset counters. Fully absorbed damage does not end Stable or add a death failure in this provisional interpretation. Independent Maiming can still cause its Wound save.\n\n**Stabilize:** an Action and DC 10 Wisdom (Medicine) check, or an Action using one healer's-kit use automatically, stabilizes a living dying creature at zero. It remains Unconscious and makes no death saves. Roll 1d4 once when it becomes Stable and record its absolute recovery time that many hours later. If still Stable then, it regains 1 HP. Damage reaching normal HP cancels that deadline and resumes dying with the appropriate failures. Rest and Stable recovery use the same elapsed clock, so four hours of recovery inside an eight-hour Watch do not cost twelve hours. A later rest does not separately replay the Stable healing event.\n\n**Dead:** ordinary healing and stabilization do not restore life. Resurrection must satisfy its own willing-soul, body, time, cost and target rules. It does not automatically remove Wounds, Corruption, debts, legal death or claims upon the body. Corpses cannot supply repeated resource triggers.",
      "role": "One Wound on the stated failure; actual zero/recipient lifecycle",
      "descriptiveTopic": "Resolve a damage event / Zero HP and recovery",
      "sectionReferenceType": "Descriptive topic; use the exact supplied path/hash and line range when present, not a claimed verbatim heading"
    },
    {
      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/COMBAT_ACTION_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 30409,
      "sha256": "f90d039142145fbd31c37992ebece70d7926dc4b6081e340f9a2fd9dc7237bc6",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 154,
        "last": 180
      },
      "evidenceText": "## Movement and geometry\n\nTrack **spent movement cost**, not distance, for ordinary turn movement. Mode cap = `(1 + number of legal Dash actions this turn) × current Speed for that mode`; remaining = max(0, cap − spent cost). Switching modes never refunds cost. Recalculate cap on Speed changes, preserving spent cost. Stand from Prone costs floor(half current Speed) and requires nonzero Speed and enough budget. Dropping Prone costs no movement.\n\nFor each foot traveled, normal/difficult/severe terrain costs 1/2/3; use strongest terrain only. Add +1 for crawling and +1 for squeezing. Crawl in difficult =3:1; crawl+squeeze in difficult =4:1. A nonhostile occupied space counts as difficult terrain. Enter a hostile creature's space only with ≥two-size difference or an express feature; never willingly end in an occupied space. Squeeze through a space one size smaller, with own attack/DEX-save disadvantage and attacks-against advantage.\n\nWalk10 through difficult spends20; switch to Fly30 leaves10. Stand with Speed30 spends15; switching to Fly30 leaves15. Two legal Dashes at Speed30 give cap90; prior expenditure still counts. Interpose/Press grant5 budget: they cannot enter a difficult 5-ft tile costing10. Their separate budgets never refill ordinary movement. Ready movement uses Speed at release; Withdraw uses half current Speed. All voluntary movement requires nonzero relevant Speed.\n\nForced movement does not use these ledgers or provoke ordinary opportunity attacks, cannot place creatures in solid matter, and stops at obstacles. Teleport does not traverse intervening terrain or spend Speed unless printed; it requires a legal destination. Speed0 alone does not block forced movement or teleport. Flight with Prone/Speed0/lost support causes Falling unless Hover or an explicit magical support exception applies; use the existing fixed Fall Marker procedure. Reaching support ends Falling.\n\nNEW grid metric: each orthogonal or diagonal neighboring tile is 5 ft; use the same metric for ordinary ranges/areas. No diagonal cut through a corner when both adjacent orthogonal sides are completely blocked. Exact templates, 3D geometry, mounts, chases, jumping, climbing/swimming procedures and currents need further source review.\n\n## Cover, sight, Hide and Search\n\nKeep **visibility**, **known location**, and **line of effect** separate. Seeing through a barrier does not itself permit attacking through it. Best cover only: half +2 AC/DEX saves; three-quarters +5; total prevents direct targeting through that obstruction. Never add +2 and +5. For areas, evaluate line/cover from the area's origin to each target; total cover there excludes a creature unless the effect explicitly travels around/through the obstruction. The origin point itself must be legally targetable.\n\nWithin normal ranged range, no range penalty; beyond normal through long, disadvantage; beyond long, illegal. Ranged attacks also have disadvantage when within5ft of a non-Incapacitated hostile that can see the attacker, even if the attacker cannot see that hostile. All advantage/disadvantage follows the single cancellation rule.\n\nNEW **Hide**: permitted only against observers unable to see you clearly or where a feature permits. Roll DEX Stealth once, record natural die and total. Against each observer's passive DC `10 + WIS Perception modifier including applicable proficiency`, use the Core four-degree task system. Meet DC succeeds before natural1/20 degree adjustment; Success/Critical Success establishes Hidden for that observer. No unsourced passive ±5 adjustment for advantage/disadvantage. Actual Search rolls use their normal modifiers.\n\nHidden means that observer cannot see you and does not know your location. Clear sight or learning your position ends Hidden for that observer. Moving between hiding places can retain Hidden only if no clear sight/revealing event occurs. A failed Hide cannot simply be rerolled unchanged; a materially new hiding position, concealment, distraction, sight-breaking event or express reroll feature is required. Hidden is not a generic immunity or a free stealth roll for every move.\n\n**Search** Action: WIS Perception for sensory detection; INT Investigation for appropriate traces/deduction; Core Arcana/Engineering where relevant. Directly locating a Hidden creature defaults to Perception against its recorded final Stealth total as a static DC, using task degrees/natural shifts. Success reveals location only to the searcher and ends Hidden for that observer, without automatically granting sight. Communicate normally to inform others. Search can be repeated on later turns by paying the Action.\n\nAttacks against unseen targets have disadvantage; unseen attackers have advantage, subject to the **Core Invisible exception**: once its position is known, Invisible's own attack modifiers end for that observer and cannot immediately return solely because it remains Invisible. Independent darkness, smoke, blindness or hiding can still supply modifiers.\n\nResolve an attack using pre-attack information; afterward reveal location to observers able to perceive its evidence. If an unseen target's location is unknown, do not directly choose it for a creature-target effect. A weapon/projectile can instead attack a suspected space: wrong space automatically misses; correct space uses unseen rules. A “creature you can see” requirement is never met by position knowledge alone. Area effects can target legal points under their own rules.",
      "role": "Real terrain/crawl/squeeze cost and forced movement",
      "descriptiveTopic": "Movement, terrain, cover and perception",
      "sectionReferenceType": "Descriptive topic; use the exact supplied path/hash and line range when present, not a claimed verbatim heading"

      "sha256": "adb2f4752b45dc70ce792a675bb30dda362aab5056588061bcde3ba31c4c8a81",

      "section": "Damage, healing, stacking and companions",

        "first": 31,

      },
      "evidenceText": "## Damage, healing, stacking and companions\n\nRoll once for each shared area/pulse and apply saves/defenses to each target separately; separate attacks roll separately. Halve once for a successful save and apply resistance/immunity normally. Same-named conditions/bonuses do not stack unless a printed cap allows it; same-stat flat bonuses from these class techniques use the strongest. Different resources remain separate. Damage reduction from these class features uses the strongest flat reduction, then applies resistance; it cannot create negative damage or healing. Temporary HP never stack; choose the larger remaining amount. Immunity is not reduced resistance.\n\nRepeated permanent HP recovery must consume finite RP, a per-rest use or another finite resource; a combat-generated gauge alone never enables unlimited healing. Healing cannot exceed maximum HP or erase Wounds, death consequences, debt or Corruption unless explicit. Downed is living at 0 HP; dead is different. Resurrection requires a willing soul and sufficiently intact body, obeys its time window, cannot create a duplicate and never refunds the feature on failure to meet known prerequisites. Sacrificial HP is actual unpreventable loss, cannot be redirected or paid with temporary HP, and is not damage for resource triggers. It cannot reduce the donor below 1 unless a card expressly grants a death-triggered effect.\n\nNo damage/healing copy repeats the source's costs, procs, resource generation, critical bonus dice, buffs, actions, cooldown resets or duration. Compute fractions once, capped at 100% of one stored source unless an explicit card states a separately priced higher amount. Copies still cause ordinary damage consequences, concentration checks and death. A copy cannot copy another copy. A target/source record must refer to an actual resolved event, not merely a known ability name.\n\nWhere a class card calls for a **standard companion** without a more specific profile, use: Small or Medium; AC 12+floor(PB/2); HP 2 L; walking Speed 30 ft; saves equal owner's PB; one melee or ranged attack (reach 5 ft/range 30 ft) at owner's class attack bonus, damage S d6 of the printed type. It has no independent class features, resources, loot, death triggers or extra turns. All companions belonging to one character share one Bonus Action command and **one damaging attack/action total per round** unless a specifically priced feature grants more. Without a command they move and Dodge. Act immediately after owner. A standard companion lasts 1 minute, disappears at 0 HP and cannot be healed for free by dismissing/resummoning. The creation cost must be paid again. Creature-specific stat blocks override this profile only where explicitly printed.",
      "role": "One strongest class flat reduction; actual stages and companion limits"
    },

      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/CORE_RESOLUTION_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 15877,
      "sha256": "69d0e64394de58c4d86a3ec57cf282978d131a1a9c1eaa21632bb7d3b72562da",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 35,
        "last": 60
      },
      "evidenceText": "## Resolve a damage event\n\nResolve each genuinely separate hit or damage event once. Do not split one packet to multiply Wound saves or combine separate hits merely to cross a threshold. Feature-specific prevention that acts before damage or before reaching zero resolves at its printed timing first.\n\n1. Resolve attack/save, damage and applicable defenses. The remaining nonnegative amount is **resolved damage**.\n2. Record HP, Wounds and zero-HP state before the event. Remove temporary HP first. **Post-temp damage = resolved damage minus temporary HP absorbed.** Keep this amount even when it exceeds current HP; floor the resulting current HP at zero.\n3. Check V6 Instant Death. If post-temp damage minus HP before the hit is at least maximum HP, the creature dies. For a creature already at zero, this is post-temp damage at least maximum HP. Stop: there is no Wound save, death-save failure or successful knockout from that event.\n4. Otherwise, a living creature makes one Wound save if it dropped from positive HP to zero, if **2 × post-temp damage is at least maximum HP**, or if the effect actually inflicts Maiming. Overlapping triggers still cause only one save. The post-temp threshold and one-save rule are new provisional interpretations. Maiming is an independent trigger even when temporary HP absorbs all ordinary damage; immunity to Maiming itself still applies.\n5. Make a Constitution save against **max(10, floor(post-temp damage / 2))**. Use pre-event Wounds for the -1-per-Wound penalty. Maiming with zero post-temp damage uses DC 10. A Wound gained now cannot penalize the roll that created it.\n6. A success adds no Wound. A failure that began with zero, one or two Wounds adds exactly one; failure by 5 or more also gives that Wound one Injury. A failure that began with three Wounds kills the creature instead: no fourth Wound and no Injury roll. Stop processing later living-target consequences if dead.\n7. If the creature was already at zero before this event and post-temp damage is positive, end Stable. A creature using death saves adds one failure, or two for a critical hit; three failures kill it. An ordinary creature that does not use death saves dies instead, without starting death counters. A hit from positive HP to zero does not also cause a same-packet death failure.\n8. If this event newly reduces positive HP to zero, a valid melee knockout makes the surviving creature Stable. Otherwise a character/significant NPC using death saves begins dying; an ordinary monster dies. A previously Stable creature whose temporary HP absorbed all damage remains Stable unless an independent Maiming result killed it. The Director identifies significant NPCs consistently. Knockout cannot override Instant Death or a fatal Wound save.\n\nCore's Maiming and Wound trigger rules remain subject to an actual feature's eligibility. The reference helper receives already legal events; it does not grant every attack Maiming or a damage-prevention exception.\n\n## Zero HP and recovery\n\nA living character at zero HP is Unconscious. Core's condition already includes Incapacitated, unawareness, dropping held items and falling Prone; an adjacent hit is critical as specified there. Healing does not silently remove other independent causes of Unconscious or Prone.\n\nWhile dying, make one death save at the **start of each regular initiative turn**. Roll d20 without modifiers unless a feature specifically modifies death saves. Natural 20 restores 1 HP; natural 1 adds two failures. Other results of 10 or more add one success; 9 or less add one failure. Three successes make the creature Stable; three failures make it Dead. Extra turns and Actions add no extra death saves. Stable or Dead creatures do not roll them.\n\nRegaining positive HP or becoming Stable resets both counters. Temporary HP does not raise normal HP, end Unconscious or reset counters. Fully absorbed damage does not end Stable or add a death failure in this provisional interpretation. Independent Maiming can still cause its Wound save.\n\n**Stabilize:** an Action and DC 10 Wisdom (Medicine) check, or an Action using one healer's-kit use automatically, stabilizes a living dying creature at zero. It remains Unconscious and makes no death saves. Roll 1d4 once when it becomes Stable and record its absolute recovery time that many hours later. If still Stable then, it regains 1 HP. Damage reaching normal HP cancels that deadline and resumes dying with the appropriate failures. Rest and Stable recovery use the same elapsed clock, so four hours of recovery inside an eight-hour Watch do not cost twelve hours. A later rest does not separately replay the Stable healing event.\n\n**Dead:** ordinary healing and stabilization do not restore life. Resurrection must satisfy its own willing-soul, body, time, cost and target rules. It does not automatically remove Wounds, Corruption, debts, legal death or claims upon the body. Corpses cannot supply repeated resource triggers.",

      "path": "DescentReviewChanges/ReviewBible/docs/mechanics/COMBAT_ACTION_PLAYTEST.md",
      "bytes": 30409,
      "sha256": "f90d039142145fbd31c37992ebece70d7926dc4b6081e340f9a2fd9dc7237bc6",
      "authority": "FROZEN_SOURCE_OR_ACCEPTED_INTERFACE",
      "lineRange": {
        "first": 45,
        "last": 70
      },
      "evidenceText": "## Time and event ordering — NEW\n\nFor a duration created in initiative, record the relevant original regular start/end marker. Existing effects keep that **virtual marker**, repeating every six seconds if the creature leaves or changes initiative. Reordering, rerolling, extra turns, death/removal or leaving/reentering initiative never restart an existing duration. New effects use the current schedule. Source death still ends concentration, a source-dependent aura/tether or an effect explicitly ending on death.\n\n- A bare hostile target-bound **N rounds** ends at the Nth future regular target-end marker. An in-progress target turn's current end does not count.\n- A bare self/source buff, ally-targeted buff or source-controlled aura **N rounds** ends at the Nth future source-start marker. Explicit target-turn, pulse or absolute timing overrides this default. Activation during source turn 1 for three rounds ends at source turn 4's start.\n- **Next turn** means the next future regular turn of the named creature, not the current turn's still-upcoming end. An unqualified next turn refers to source/user under the existing shared rule. Explicit “this turn” can include the current turn.\n- An outside-initiative bare N-round effect instead gets an absolute application-time + N×6 seconds deadline. Entering initiative preserves that deadline.\n- Explicit periodic profiles retain their registered clocks and tick counts. Never create a second timer merely because a generic duration rule also exists.\n\nThe [exact GPT review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_EXACT_FINDINGS.md) confirms ally-buff source clocks and excluding the current turn for explicit next-turn endpoints. The helper excludes a specific current-end marker only if that marker would otherwise be selected: application during a turn at time1 with end3 excludes3 and selects9; application at/after3 also selects9, without erroneously skipping again to15.\n\nAt a regular turn's **start**, resolve in this order:\n\n1. Expire start-ending effects and apply immediate state changes from those expirations.\n2. Refresh Reaction; tick regular-start recharge/non-action clocks.\n3. Resolve already-due absolute state recovery if now legal, including a previously blocked Stable recovery deadline.\n4. If still alive, dying and at 0 HP with death saves, make that turn's death save.\n5. Resolve other start effects and periodic events in application order.\n6. Begin voluntary turn decisions.\n\nAt **end**, resolve triggered effects, due damage, saves and maintenance before end-ending effects expire; then the slot finishes. No extra turn creates another regular marker. The caller must not reuse a resolved marker.\n\nFor simultaneous effects, earlier application timestamp precedes later. For simultaneous components of one source, its printed internal order controls; otherwise use stable application sequence, with source/mechanical ID as a final tie-break. Record that sequence when creating the effects. Beneficial/harmful labels do not change priority. Periodic family arbitration happens once per family/marker before its damage, retaining the family registry's consumed loser ticks.\n\nExamples: dying at 0 rolls its death save before ordinary start-turn healing; a fatal death save is not undone by ordinary healing. Stable at 0 skips the save, then start-turn damage can break Stable. A natural 20 can restore 1 HP before a due Bleed drops it to 0 as a new drop event. If Decay expires while a Stable recovery deadline is already due, the old deadline resolves once without rerolling recovery time.",

Pure transfer/redirection keeps the original root's nullNonlethal flag. It does not create a second root or extra prevention/resource triggers. Snapshot recipient identity/lifecycle, pre-event HP/tempHP/Wounds/death state and the root nullNonlethal flag. Resolve ordinary defenses, resistance and temp-HP absorption and calculate the ordinary post-temp damage/HP loss values; attached non-damage riders remain pending under their own triggers.\n2. If nullNonlethal is false, use the ordinary CORE/HEALING_DEATH/lifecycle procedure unchanged. If true, continue below. Objects/noncreature structures still take ordinary object damage and can be destroyed; the word nonlethal does not make objects indestructible.\n3. For any living-recipient qualifying damage packet, suppress damage-caused Instant Death and suppress the packet’s Core Wound/Maiming save entirely, including half-maximum and positive-HP Wound triggers. Therefore the packet cannot create a fatal fourth-Wound result while the recipient remains above 0 or reaches 0. Existing Wounds remain recorded.\n4. Apply normal HP loss down to minimum 0. Positive→positive: stop the Null zero-state branch; recipient keeps resulting positive HP, existing Wounds/conditions and independent riders. Already Stable0: causes no failure/death and keeps the existing recovery deadline exactly; never reroll/extend it. The Null override replaces only the damage-caused lethal/destruction endpoint named by that branch. Independent duration, dismissal, concentration/source end, sacrifice/cost, Hunt finality or other non-damage expiry remains real.\n7. Would-die/death-trigger protections do not trigger or spend if their prerequisite death/fatal branch was skipped by Null. A source-specific trigger keyed only to damage reducing HP to0 without killing, such as actual Undying Spellform, still triggers. Independent non-damage riders resolve if their own trigger remains true and they do not require a Wound/death result.\n8. Direct HP payments/sacrificial costs are not damage and are never converted.       "evidenceText": "## Movement and geometry\n\nTrack **spent movement cost**, not distance, for ordinary turn movement. Dropping Prone costs no movement.\n\nFor each foot traveled, normal/difficult/severe terrain costs 1/2/3; use strongest terrain only. Add +1 for crawling and +1 for squeezing. Crawl in difficult =3:1; crawl+squeeze in difficult =4:1. A nonhostile occupied space counts as difficult terrain. Enter a hostile creature's space only with ≥two-size difference or an express feature; never willingly end in an occupied space. Squeeze through a space one size smaller, with own attack/DEX-save disadvantage and attacks-against advantage.\n\nWalk10 through difficult spends20; switch to Fly30 leaves10. All voluntary movement requires nonzero relevant Speed.\n\nForced movement does not use these ledgers or provoke ordinary opportunity attacks, cannot place creatures in solid matter, and stops at obstacles. Teleport does not traverse intervening terrain or spend Speed unless printed; it requires a legal destination. Speed0 alone does not block forced movement or teleport. Flight with Prone/Speed0/lost support causes Falling unless Hover or an explicit magical support exception applies; use the existing fixed Fall Marker procedure. Reaching support ends Falling.\n\nNEW grid metric: each orthogonal or diagonal neighboring tile is 5 ft; use the same metric for ordinary ranges/areas. No diagonal cut through a corner when both adjacent orthogonal sides are completely blocked. Exact templates, 3D geometry, mounts, chases, jumping, climbing/swimming procedures and currents need further source review.\n\n## Cover, sight, Hide and Search\n\nKeep **visibility**, **known location**, and **line of effect** separate. Seeing through a barrier does not itself permit attacking through it. Best cover only: half +2 AC/DEX saves; three-quarters +5; total prevents direct targeting through that obstruction. Never add +2 and +5. For areas, evaluate line/cover from the area's origin to each target; total cover there excludes a creature unless the effect explicitly travels around/through the obstruction. The origin point itself must be legally targetable.\n\nWithin normal ranged range, no range penalty; beyond normal through long, disadvantage; beyond long, illegal. Ranged attacks also have disadvantage when within5ft of a non-Incapacitated hostile that can see the attacker, even if the attacker cannot see that hostile. All advantage/disadvantage follows the single cancellation rule.\n\nNEW **Hide**: permitted only against observers unable to see you clearly or where a feature permits. Roll DEX Stealth once, record natural die and total. Against each observer's passive DC `10 + WIS Perception modifier including applicable proficiency`, use the Core four-degree task system. Meet DC succeeds before natural1/20 degree adjustment; Success/Critical Success establishes Hidden for that observer. No unsourced passive ±5 adjustment for advantage/disadvantage. Actual Search rolls use their normal modifiers.\n\nHidden means that observer cannot see you and does not know your location. Clear sight or learning your position ends Hidden for that observer. Moving between hiding places can retain Hidden only if no clear sight/revealing event occurs. A failed Hide cannot simply be rerolled unchanged; a materially new hiding position, concealment, distraction, sight-breaking event or express reroll feature is required. Hidden is not a generic immunity or a free stealth roll for every move.\n\n**Search** Action: WIS Perception for sensory detection; INT Investigation for appropriate traces/deduction; Core Arcana/Engineering where relevant. Directly locating a Hidden creature defaults to Perception against its recorded final Stealth total as a static DC, using task degrees/natural shifts. Success reveals location only to the searcher and ends Hidden for that observer, without automatically granting sight. Communicate normally to inform others. Search can be repeated on later turns by paying the Action.\n\nAttacks against unseen targets have disadvantage; unseen attackers have advantage, subject to the **Core Invisible exception**: once its position is known, Invisible's own attack modifiers end for that observer and cannot immediately return solely because it remains Invisible. Independent darkness, smoke, blindness or hiding can still supply modifiers.\n\nResolve an attack using pre-attack information; afterward reveal location to observers able to perceive its evidence. If an unseen target's location is unknown, do not directly choose it for a creature-target effect. A weapon/projectile can instead attack a suspected space: wrong space automatically misses; correct space uses unseen rules. A “creature you can see” requirement is never met by position knowledge alone. Area effects can target legal points under their own rules.",

## Preserved original scenario texts

### EV4-02-A

EV4-02-A: A creature succeeds against raw force 37 and takes floor(37/2)=18 before force resistance, which then gives 9. No attack critical or later orb is created.

### EV4-02-B

EV4-02-B: An unattended ordinary chest has a real HP/material profile but no DEX-save profile, so it fails and receives the one 37-force event under those defenses. Destroying it does not also strike the creature behind it or erase an artifact inside.

### EV4-04-A

EV4-04-A: A lightning-immune creature takes zero from raw 27 but fails the independent CON save. If Stunned is otherwise legal and the shared hard guard is unused, it becomes Stunned and the allowance is spent. Another target can take the lightning while its already-used guard blocks only Stunned.

### EV4-04-B

EV4-04-B: Applied during the target’s round-4 turn, the hostile endpoint is its round-5 regular END, not the round-4 END or an intervening extra turn. Suppression during round 5 preserves that deadline; when it expires, no later resumption or restoration of lost concentration occurs.

### EV5-03-A

EV5-03-A: A cold-immune creature fails the legal Mage’s DC16 CON save. Its5d8 cold event is reduced to0, but it has no Paralyzed immunity and the source’s hard-control allowance is unused. Paralyzed establishes and spends that allowance; it ends at the second future regular target END, not at the END of an already-underway turn.

### EV5-03-B

EV5-03-B: The same Mage has already established whole-Action denial against this target this encounter. A separate paid Frozen Tomb still resolves its CON save and5d8 cold; failure with raw23 deals23 before applicable defenses. Paralyzed is blocked by the spent allowance. No Frozen, Speed0 substitute, new denial budget or recovered10RP is supplied.

### EV5-04-A

EV5-04-A: Shared fire dice total29 and bludgeoning dice11. A target succeeds on DEX, so its raw typed amounts are14fire and5bludgeoning. Fire resistance halves14 to7; bludgeoning remains5. The resulting12 is one combined damage event, not two concentration checks merely because two types survived.

### EV5-04-B

EV5-04-B: An ally and a perceived designated foe share the prism, while another foe is behind a solid wall. Only the eligible perceived foe with line of effect is a recipient. A failed save deals the two real typed components; impale does not pin it, leave ammunition, damage its carried armor or expose the foe behind the wall.

### EV5-05-A

EV5-05-A: Six perceived hostile creatures are in range and have line of effect. All six are included. Shared6d8 is27; a successful CON save gives13 before defenses and lightning resistance then gives6. A failed-save lightning-immune target takes0 and receives no Stunned or secondary rider.

### EV5-05-B

EV5-05-B: An unseen creature behind total cover, a friendly-looking deceiver not designated hostile, and a foe45feet away are not selected. A metal chain linking them to a struck foe creates no new target. The Mage still pays the real10RP and gains ordinary Dissonance; damaging several enemies grants no spell-defined refund.

### EV5-06-A

EV5-06-A: The cone is aimed along a clear horizontal axis. At30feet along it, its radius is15feet. An ally whose actual body intersects that region is included along with foes. Shared8d8 is35; a successful CON save takes17 before defenses, and cold resistance reduces that amount to8.

### EV5-06-B

EV5-06-B: A creature wholly beyond the far60foot cap or shielded by total cover from the origin receives no damage. A later entrant is unaffected. Water, rations and the caster’s carried equipment gain no frozen/preserved/damaged state from this creature-only damage procedure, even when adjacent to a struck target.

### EV5-08-A

EV5-08-A: The Mage chooses radiant before casting. Shared10d6 is37. One ally in the60foot sphere succeeds on CON, takes18 before defenses and stays standing. A radiant-immune foe fails CON and takes0 damage but becomes Prone because it has no applicable condition immunity.

### EV5-08-B

EV5-08-B: A flying foe with no hover fails the save and receives the selected thunder packet plus Prone under its actual rules, including any resulting ordinary fall. A hover-capable creature retains the benefit of its real hover exception. Neither receives a ten-foot push or an extra invented wave-impact packet; the selected10d6 is never doubled into20d6.

### EV6-01-A

EV6-01-A: Three perceived enemies are 20, 40 and 60 feet from the caster with line of effect. The shared roll is 29 fire. The first fails CON and takes 29 before defenses, plus the visual Blinded rider. The second succeeds and takes floor(29/2) = 14 with no rider. The third fails but is fire-immune: it takes 0 fire and can still be Blinded if its sight perceived the flash. The one cast costs 13 RP, leaving the baseline Wizard 27.

### EV6-01-B

EV6-01-B: An enemy concealed behind total effect-blocking cover is absent from the initial area snapshot even if the blast is bright. Another enemy is perceived through a genuine nonvisual sense but has no functioning visual sense: it makes the damage save and receives no visual blindness. The spell does not reveal a hidden hostile, ignite the cover or expand its target list after another event removes that cover.

### EV6-05-A

EV6-05-A: The primary is 50 feet away. Three perceived secondaries are 15, 25 and 30 feet from it, with actual arc line of effect; the last is 80 feet from the caster. That last target is legal under the explicit secondary-range rule. A shared roll of 43 gives 43 before defenses on a failed DEX save or 21 on success. The lightning-immune primary takes zero, but the three established arcs still resolve their own saves and defenses.

### EV6-05-B

EV6-05-B: Only two secondary identities are declared. One goes behind total effect-blocking cover during the cast interrupt, so only the other remains in the snapshot. After the primary is killed, that remaining arc still resolves once. The lost target is not replaced by a newly noticed enemy, the two large occupied squares of one creature cannot be two recipients, and neither secondary produces further arcs.

### EV6-07-A

EV6-07-A: A 60-foot cylinder contains two enemies and an ally; all three are area recipients. The shared roll is 27 radiant. A successful CON recipient takes 13 before defenses and no blindness. A failed-save radiant-immune recipient takes zero radiant but can still receive the visual Blinded record. Its immunity cannot silently erase a separate rider, and the ally is not exempted after target selection.

### EV6-07-B

EV6-07-B: The caster uses an Action and 13 RP for one beam, ending at 27 RP from the baseline 40. There is no concentration handle granting another beam next turn. A creature behind a line-of-effect-blocking wall is not included, even if another simultaneous event later breaks the wall. A genuinely sightless included creature still makes the damage save but does not acquire visually delivered blindness.

### EV6-08-A

EV6-08-A: The center is 60 feet from the caster and the radius is another 60 feet, so eligible bodies may extend to 120 feet from the caster. A shared 10d6 roll of 37 gives 37 cold on a failed CON save or 18 on success before defenses. The caster is included if its actual body intersects the sphere with center line of effect. At the same snapshot, an exposed one-foot-deep pond patch freezes; blood, a sealed enemy waterskin and later river flow do not become new water targets.

### EV6-08-B

EV6-08-B: A qualifying five-foot ice section has 20 structural HP and supports an actual local 400-lb load under the default 500-lb profile. Its current ice lot weighs 1,000 lb by the recorded source-water inventory. Fire removes 5 structural HP after its vulnerability, melting 5/20 of that section: 250 lb water and 750 lb remaining ice, with no added clean water or new stock. The split portions retain contamination and elapsed warm-exposure history. If the original water was temporary Conjure Water, its original expiration still removes the remaining source-owned matter.

### IN4-11-A

IN4-11-A: A documented Plant creature receives one 10d8 roll of 47. A successful CON save gives 23; necrotic resistance then gives 11 before other actual prevention. It is neither automatically destroyed nor assigned a separate second hit for the extra two dice.

### IN4-11-B

IN4-11-B: A nonplant creature wearing wooden armor receives the ordinary 8d8 procedure, and its armor takes no automatic damage. A separately tracked living shrub can qualify for the mundane-plant mode; a crate of already harvested herbs cannot, and neither outcome generates a new harvest claim.

### IN6-10-A

IN6-10-A: A shared 6d6 roll is 25. A living ordinary creature that succeeds on CON takes 12 necrotic before defenses and no blindness. An actual Undead that fails and is necrotic-immune takes zero damage but still attempts the separate Blinded rider. If it is also Blinded-immune, that rider is prevented by its own defense rather than by the zero damage.

### IN6-10-B

IN6-10-B: A failed-save Undead is struck during its current regular turn. The proposed blindness endpoint is its next future regular END, excluding the current turn's END. A later extra turn does not advance that endpoint. An ordinary cure of Blinded can end the condition earlier, but neither cure nor suppression refunds the already-resolved damage or the caster's 13 RP.

### IN6-12-A

IN6-12-A: The caster places the center 60 feet away. The sphere has radius 60, so an exposed creature at the far boundary can be 120 feet from the caster. A shared roll of 29 necrotic becomes 14 on a successful CON save before resistance. The caster is included if its own occupied space is within the real sphere; allies are not selectively omitted.

### IN6-12-B

IN6-12-B: A creature behind total cover from the center receives no packet, while a currently unseen creature with area line of effect does. An Undead with necrotic immunity takes zero through that defense, not through a blanket type exclusion. The resulting corpse of a creature genuinely killed by damage is not additionally disintegrated or made unusable for resurrection solely by this spell's title.

### EN5-04-A

EN5-04-A: An enemy within20feet finishes an ordinary attack Action. Wizard9 legally reacts, pays32→22RP and rolls4d6=15. A target fails INT, taking15before defenses, then elects a legal Counterspell Reaction: its Reaction and the debt are consumed, but Counterspell is not cast and pays noRP. This cannot undo the attack that triggered Mental Bomb.

### EN5-04-B

EN5-04-B: The triggering enemy had just completed an opportunity-attack Reaction. It fails against Mental Bomb, but that completed attack is unchanged. At its next regular START its ordinary Reaction refreshes; the next legal election before that future END can be denied. If none is elected, the record expires with no later banked loss.

### IL5-02-A

IL5-02-A: The target fails WIS;4d6rolls15and its actual psychic resistance reduces that to7. Its independent fear immunity prevents Frightened, but does not erase the7damage. The caster learns no private memory or fear-image description from that result.

### IL5-02-B

IL5-02-B: An admitted Frightened target loses sight of the caster, so current condition restrictions use that real sight state; the spell does not create an omnipresent visible monster. It rolls17at the next eligible END and ends this application. There is no second4d6tick from that turn or an extra turn.

### IL5-04-A

IL5-04-A: The shared5d6rollis19. A successful WIS save takes9before defenses and receives no instruction. A failed-save creature immune only to psychic damage takes0but can still receive the movement instruction; fear immunity by itself does not block this non-Frightened effect.

### IL5-04-B

IL5-04-B: At execution a target has30movement available on ordinary difficult terrain. Increasing-distance travel pays2perfoot, so at most15actual feet are possible before that budget ends. An earlier valid compulsion that already used20leaves only10budget, permitting5feet here; the swarm does not supply a second30.

### IN6-04-A

IN6-04-A: The caster and three allies are within the actual 30-foot sphere along with two foes. All six eligible creatures make DEX; the caster is not selectively omitted. A failed-save creature with Speed 30 and enough movement can later spend 15 movement to stand. An existing Prone creature receives no second posture or extra standing charge.

### IN6-04-B

IN6-04-B: A flying enemy fails DEX and lacks Hover or another support exception. Its new Prone condition starts ordinary Falling through the existing Fall Marker; the spell itself deals no water impact or fall damage. A nearby hovering creature can be Prone without losing its actual hover support. Seeing that the wave is false does not automatically block the proposed balance disruption.
